ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-22-sejm-23-4.u110	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! I don't want to go into polemics with all the voices that were quoted here, nor do I want to take part in the bidding, who was first. Law and Justice submitted some kind of project on February 10th. It is appropriate to thank all those who contributed to this, of all clubs, because in this discussion, which was long, difficult, there were a lot of people involved. I believe that if not everyone, almost everyone was motivated by good intentions and a desire to find the best solution. I'd like to thank you all very much for that. We will adopt an important resolution - a resolution, which will at least partially help us to make up for the lost last twenty-several years, during which the Polish state could not properly pay tribute to Poles and Polish citizens of other nationalities murdered in Volyn and Kresy South-East II of the Republic. Today we will honor them and establish a celebration, the National Day of Remembrance of Victims of Genocide by Ukrainian nationalists on the citizens of the Second Republic of Poland. High Izbo! During World War II, the most dramatic devastations were made by two of the greatest totalitarianisms of the 20th century, i.e. German fascism and Soviet communism, but Poles living in Kres were also victims of another, particularly cruel crime: the crimes of genocide committed by Ukrainian nationalists. It was a particularly painful experience because the perpetrators of this crime were not strangers, but neighbors who lived with us under the same sky for centuries. The apogee of these dramatic events, which began in 1939 and continued until 1947, took place in the years 1943-1945. It is the crimes of that time that are commonly referred to by all Polish historians and prosecutors as genocide. They are classified as genocide, i.e. conscious, planned action, aimed at destroying a particular national group, in this case Poles. To those who, contrary to the views of historians and prosecutors, would have doubts about the nature of these dramatic events, subjecting to a lying narrative about some Polish-Ukrainian war, about the alleged bottom-up break of the simple Ukrainian population, it is worth recalling the words of one of Ukrainian ideologists Mikhail Kołodziński, a member of the highest authorities of the Ukrainian Nationalist Organization. He's already in the second half of the 30s. In the document of the Ukrainian military doctrine, he wrote directly about actions that should be directed at the Polish population. He wrote: The Polish element, which will resist actively, must fall into battle, and the rest must be terrorized and forced to flee for the Vistula, because it is impossible for the Polish element to live here alongside the Ukrainians after the victory. It must be remembered that the more Polish element dies during the uprising, the easier it will be to build a Ukrainian state. And further: In the face of the hostile element, such cruelty must be shown during the uprising, to make ten more. the generation was afraid to even look towards Ukraine. Sadly, such texts can be quoted much more. By adopting this resolution, you have to ask yourself questions. First of all, do we need a holiday, a National Day of Remembrance of the Victims of Genocide, and second of all, or do we not show a lack of sensitivity, or do we harm our neighbours in Ukraine fighting Russian aggression in the east? These questions came out here in the O.R. today. Contrary to appearances, the answer to both of these questions is not very difficult. July 11th is the anniversary of bloody Sunday, July 11, 1943, when the real apogee of slaughter occurred. Nearly 100 Polish villages that day were attacked by Ukrainian nationalists, several thousand people were killed. These murdered memories simply belong, as usual in the world, in human terms. [[Applause]] Just as the Sejm established another holiday a few years ago, the National Remembrance Day of the Victims of the Nazi German Concentration Camps, so today we have a chance to pay tribute to the victims of Volyn and the South-East Kres. We restore the murdered and those dramatic events of our national memory not to bear hatred in our hearts, but to never repeat such a drama again. That is how Pope Francis justified the need to speak openly about the crime of genocide during his recent pilgrimage to Armenia. You have to talk about it so it never happens again. [[Applause]] Second question, High Izbo. Aren't we showing too little sensitivity? Aren't we going to hurt our neighbours in Ukraine fighting Russian aggression in the east of the country? Don't we support our actions of Russian propaganda? High Izbo, since the first days of the revolution of dignity Poland supports Ukraine, which defends itself against Russian aggression. We do this as Poland at both diplomatic and financial level, but supporting our neighbour in the fight for independence does not rule out dialogue, but also strengthens the need to conduct it. Dialogue must be based on truth. Supporting Ukraine in no way implies consent to the glorifying of murderers and troops responsible for carrying out genocide. [[Applause]] It does not mean silence in a situation where the memory of Polish victims is defiled, and today such a process unfortunately takes place. A group of Ukrainian ideologists is trying to build a modern Ukrainian national identity based on the ethos of the Ukrainian Nationalist Organization and the Ukrainian Insurgent Army. Further streets and squares are given the names of commanders of these organizations - commanders directly responsible for crimes committed in Poles. How can we keep quiet about this? After all, it is unacceptable for ethical reasons - after all, it is not acceptable to glorify murderers - as well as for pragmatic reasons, because we will not build anything long-term on lying, manipulating and glorifying evil. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ukraine is a sovereign country today. Let us remind you that in December 1991 Poland was the first country to recognise its independence. In 2007 the Commission The IPN has published a "Kress Book of the Righteous..." which contains a list of hundreds of Ukrainians who, risking their lives, and sometimes and consequently losing them, rescued their Polish neighbours from the nationalists from the CNS and UPA, who demonstrated themselves in these terrible times of humanity and decency. It is in this resolution that we thank them, that we appeal to the President of the Republic of Poland today for their award. [[Applause]] And it is today before the Ukrainian political class that there is a choice: whether bandits and criminals, or the Righteous, who in these terrible times gave evidence of humanity and Christian mercy to the young generation of Ukrainians, saving their neighbors. This respect for the dead is our Christian duty and at the same time one of the foundations of our European civilization. Let me read the text of the resolution. [[The Gathered Rise Up]] The resolution of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland on paying tribute to the victims of genocide by Ukrainian nationalists on the citizens of the Second Republic of Poland in the years 1943-1945 The lands of the former eastern provinces of the Republic of Poland were particularly strongly experienced during World War II. In these areas the two largest totalitarianisms of the 20th century were rubbed away: the Third German Reich and the Communist Soviet Union. The actions of German and Soviet invaders created favourable conditions for the awakening of hatred against national and religious background, and attempts by representatives of the Polish Underground State to reach an agreement with Ukrainian organizations did not result. In July 2016, the 73rd anniversary of the apogee of the crime, which was carried out on the civilian population of the Eastern Kresów II of the Republic of Poland operating in those areas by troops of the Ukrainian Nationalist Organizations, the Ukrainian Uprising Army, as well as divisions of the SS Galizien and other Ukrainian formations cooperating with Germany. As a result of the genocide committed in the years 1943-1945 more than one hundred thousand citizens of the Second Republic of Poland were murdered, mainly peasants. Their exact number is still unknown, and many of them still have not received a worthy burial and commemoration. Among the murdered beside Poles were Jews, Armenians, Czechs, representatives of other national minorities, as well as Ukrainians who took the side of the victims. Recalling this crime of Ukrainian nationalists, it is impossible to silence or relativize Polish retaliation actions against Ukrainian villages, as a result of which the civilian population was also killed. All these tragic events should be restored to the memory of modern generations. Victims of crimes committed in the 1940s by Ukrainian nationalists have not yet been properly commemorated, and mass murders have not been named according to the historical truth as genocide. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland pays tribute to all citizens of the Second Republic of Poland who were brutally murdered by Ukrainian nationalists. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland expresses its highest appreciation for the soldiers of the National Army, Kresowa Samoobrona, Battalion Chłopski, who have engaged in a heroic fight to defend the civilian population at risk, and calls on the President of the Republic of Poland to honor these people with national decorations. Therefore, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland establishes on 11 July - the anniversary of the apogee of crime - the National Day of Remembrance of Victims of Genocide by Ukrainian nationalists on citizens of the Second Republic of Poland. The Sejm calls for the establishment of crime scenes and their identification, the provision of a worthy burial to all the victims found, the devotion of honor and respect due to the innocently tortured and murdered, the drawing up of a full list of victims. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland calls for the continuation of the work of reconciliation and dialogue initiated by political and spiritual leaders, supporting the cooperation of historians, including the extension of access to state archives, strengthening cooperation between the authorities of the Republic of Poland and Ukraine in the most important matters for the future of both nations. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland expresses the respect and gratitude of Ukrainians, who risked their lives, saved Poles, and calls on the President of the Republic of Poland to honor these people with State decorations. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland also resembles the attitude of a large part of the Ukrainian population, which refused to participate in the attacks on Poles. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland thankes the Kresowians and their descendants and people of good will who have been demanding the truth for decades, following the motto 'Not revenge, but memory call for sacrifice'. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland expresses its solidarity with Ukraine fighting external aggression to preserve territorial integrity. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland believes that only the full truth about history is the best way to reconciliation and mutual forgiveness. As he said in 2001 in Lviv, St. John Paul II 'Let all be ready, through the purification of historical memory, to put higher what unites than what divides, to build together a future based on mutual respect, brotherly cooperation and genuine solidarity'. High Izbo! As in 2005 we adopted a resolution to pay tribute to the Armenian genocide by acclamation, so today I appeal to the High House to adopt the resolution of Volyn. [[Long-term applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-22-sejm-23-4.u272	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In December, on behalf of the government, I presented a programme of action by the agricultural department in the field of agricultural policy for the years 2015 and 2019, i.e. by the end of the current parliamentary term. There were several important areas within this programme, and I will remind you: international affairs, rural development, economic competitiveness, land management or consolidation of agricultural institutions, including education, education and agricultural advice. In these areas the government and the resort are working, preparing a number of solutions, which were also presented in May during the presentation of the report on the half-year activity of the Ministry. Today we decided... And here I would like to thank the High House for the opportunity to provide information on the current situation in the basic agricultural markets, the arrangements at European Union level for supporting agricultural markets and the work on the development programme of the main agricultural markets in Poland for the years 2016 I asked for this right now, in July, when we are in the pre-harvest period and at the peak of milk production. We are also following the presentation by the European Commission after our long so-called procedures. July's agricultural package. I'll discuss it later in my speech. I will start with a brief presentation of the most important information on the current situation on agricultural markets. Let me focus on the milk market at first. Since the creation of our government, we have been struggling with supply-demand imbalances in this market. It is the result of increased milk production and supply of dairy products both on the domestic market and on the Union market. This is the consequence of the abolition of the milk quota system combined with the weakened demand for dairy products on the markets of developing countries and the Russian embargo on agri-food products, including dairy products, from the European Union. Poland belongs to countries from the so-called. the European Union's green belt, which has excellent conditions for the development of milk production. Let me remind you that the European Commission itself has encouraged us to develop this production, giving us bright visions and presenting optimistic forecasts of market developments in the world. As a result, Poland together with several other countries, such as the Netherlands, Germany, Ireland, is creating a leading feature of its future in developing excellent milk quality. In the first five months of 2016, Polish dairy farmers concentrated about 4.7 billion kg of milk, i.e. 6.7% more than in the same period last year. The high dynamics of milk procurement resulted in an increased production of all milk products, in particular milk powder and butter, but the continuing low prices of milk products led to a decrease in milk prices in the first quarter of 2016 even though the volume of Polish exports of dairy products increased. The buying-in price for milk has fallen by nearly 11% since the beginning of this year. , to a level below 103 PLN per 100 kg in May 2016 The milk buying-in price is 25% lower than in 2014. The national milk buying-in price expressed in euro is lower by around 11,3% in relation to the estimated average in the European Union. Indeed, the price quotations of June this year. point to a slight improvement in the prices of dairy products, which may be a preliminary indication of the recovery of the situation on the milk market, but short-term forecasts suggest that the downward trend in milk prices may persist for at least a few months. Let us hope that the milk market support package negotiated by me after long and difficult discussions will allow a reversal of this trend as soon as possible. Let us now move on to the situation in the meat markets. The situation in the beef sector is stable. The average purchase price for bovine animals is PLN 6.1 per kilogram, higher by 3.5% compared to last year. We record very good export results. In the poultrymeat sector, there is another year of increasing supply. Prices of both the buying-in of chickens and the sale of carcasses are at the price level of the previous year. Also in the poultrymeat sector, excellent export results are noted. During the first four months of 2016, poultry meat exports amounted to 306 000. t and was 16% bigger than the year before. The situation in the pigmeat sector has clearly improved in recent months. Unfortunately, the prolonged crisis has affected the decline of pig population. However, the current purchase price of pork live in Poland is PLN 5.41 per kilogram and compared to the same period last year is higher by 22%. The current price is around 5% higher than the price from 2011 to 2015. Growing trends are observed throughout the European Union, mainly due to a reduction in the supply of pigmeat and very good demand on the Chinese market, where exports of pigmeat from the European Union increased by 106% this year compared to last year. Despite the restrictions resulting from African swine fever in Poland, Polish entrepreneurs also increased their exports of pigmeat. Between January-April 2016 export of pork meat from Poland increased by 10% compared to last year, to a level of 142 thousand. a. At that time, imports also increased, but the dynamics of its growth were smaller. Now let's move on to plant production. The forecasts of the Institute of Agricultural Economics and Food Economy show that the total cereal harvest in the 2016-2017 season will amount to more than 30 million tons compared to less than 28 million tons in the previous season. High global wheat stocks and good forecasts of this year's harvest in Poland and in the world affect the development of grain prices in the country at a relatively low level. In support of cereal prices in Poland there is a low level of domestic stocks, but the price level of cereals is much higher than the intervention price, so the supply of cereals to intervention is not expected in the near future. Poland has become a significant EU cereal exporter in recent years, effectively competing with third country markets. The competitiveness of Polish cereals is influenced by good quality and affordable price. Between January and April 2016, approximately 1.7 million tons of grain were exported from the country compared to 2.5 million tons in the same period of the previous year. During this period 0.4 million t grain was imported to the country compared to 0.2 million t in the previous year. According to preliminary estimates, the total area of rape and turnip rape is expected to be reduced by around 19% this year. , up to about 0.8 million ha. The area of rape and winter turnip rape ploughed and eligible for ploughing was estimated to be around 136 thousand. ha, i.e. 16% the area sown. According to the estimates of the Institute of Agricultural Economics and Food Economy, the estimated size of the rape harvest in 2016 will be 2350 a. As far as the sugar market is concerned, the area of sugar beet sowing in Poland is around 201 000. ha and is about 17% bigger than a year ago. The increase in sowing area was due to the dreaming of winter crops in some regions of the country and the increasing cultivation of out-of-quota sugar by sugar companies. Plant sowings and risings were carried out in favorable weather conditions. If the yield is average and is around 60 t per hectare, sugar beet can be harvested at around 12.1 million t, i.e. 28% More than a year ago. The high supply of raw material will result in the production of 1.8-1.9 million tonnes of sugar, which will increase by 400-500 thousand. t from the production quota and by around 100 and 200 000. t from domestic consumption. Sugar beet shall be contracted at a minimum buying-in price of EUR 26,29 per tonne. The depreciation of the gold will cause the minimum buying-in price expressed in national currency to rise to PLN 115.116 per tonne, against PLN 110 per tonne in 2015. The level of buying-in prices will also depend on sugar prices in the country. In May 2016, the net sugar price was PLN 2.27 per kilogram, i.e. approx. 38% more than a year ago. Significantly higher sugar prices compared to last year translate into better financial results for the sugar industry. Now let's look at the fruit and vegetables market. In 2015, the total fruit harvest amounted to 4.1 million tons and was 2.1% lower than the 2014 harvest. The apple harvest amounted to 3.2 million t. In the 2016 fruit production forecasts, the Institute of Agricultural Economics and Food Economy indicates a possible small increase in total fruit harvests. Since January 2016, the prices of apples - both consumer and processing - have shown a downward trend. In the middle of this year, apple prices were more than half lower than in 2015. The fall in industrial apples prices was largely due to the high supply of less quality fruit. Large supply and lower prices of apple juice on the international market have further contributed to the fall in apple prices for processing. Lower demand for apples on the domestic market as well as lower bid prices from the largest foreign customers also resulted in a decrease in consumer apples prices. The area of vegetable cultivation was slightly smaller in 2015 than in the previous year and amounted to 176.4 thousand. ha against 178 thousand. The Commission therefore concludes that the measures provided for in this Decision are in accordance with the opinion of the Standing Committee on Plants, Animals, Food and Feed, Last year's drought has had a major impact on the yield and quality of vegetables, which also translates into greater losses during their storage. Total vegetable harvest in 2015 was by more than 13% lower than in 2014 and amounted to around 4.8 million tons. I would like to remind you that the situation of producers of fruit and vegetables improves the mechanisms of extraordinary support adopted in connection with the introduction by the Russian Federation of an embargo on imports of certain agricultural products from the European Union. In Poland, under the first three mechanisms, which included operations limiting the supply of fruit and vegetables, carried out from September 2014 to June 2015, the aid was granted in the amount of PLN 311 million. The total amount of fruit and vegetables, which as a result of the operation did not reach the domestic market, amounted to over 300,000. t. The total amount of support provided under the fourth mechanism will be around EUR 137.3 million. Legislative work is currently underway on the implementation of the fifth mechanism. Mr. Marshal! After presenting the situation on the main agricultural markets, I would now like to discuss some initiatives taken by the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development to improve and stabilise the situation on agricultural markets. I will start with the draft law on countering unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage in trade in agricultural and food products, which has already been adopted by the Council of Ministers. The food supply chain is a very important element of both national and global economies, and its importance is not only the economic dimension but, above all, the social dimension. For the proper functioning of the food supply chain, it is essential to maintain a good relationship between all its links. The draft law sets out rules against practices consisting in unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage by one of the parties to the contract. It covers suppliers of agricultural or food products as well as buyers of these products, protecting them from the use by one of the parties of unfair use of the contract advantage. The draft law also amends the Act on the Agricultural Market Agency, introducing penalties for the purchase of agricultural products without a written agreement. The next point I wanted to talk about is the so-called concept. Polish product. In the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, a draft law was drawn up on amending the Act on the Commercial Quality of Agricultural and Food Products, which proposed the introduction of new regulations on voluntary labelling of foodstuffs with information about the Polish product, together with the designation of the design of the mark containing this information. These regulations will give producers the opportunity to indicate in the labelling of foodstuffs the national origin of the food produced in respect of not only the place of production but also the raw materials used. On 19 July, the draft law was adopted by the Council of Ministers. In addition, the project will be notified to the European Commission. We are also working on rules on the labelling of meat from the country of origin. In response to signals about the need to increase consumers' access to information on the origin of food products, legislative proposals are being drawn up in the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development on an area that is not regulated on the EU market. They refer to the obligation to provide country of origin information in the case of meat of pigs, poultry, sheep and goats sold without packaging. Let me remind you that the obligation to mark the country of origin of meat sold in packaging already exists. The draft rules are at the end of the inter-ministerial arrangements. I would also like to inform you that the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development is actively involved in the work on the Responsible Development Strategy prepared by the Minister for Development. With regard to agricultural markets, it has been notified, inter alia, a project for the launch of the food platform. The implementation of the project will allow for the concentration of supply of domestic agri-food products on the European and global markets, which will allow for the creation of large, homogeneous lots. It will also reduce transaction costs and commercial risks, including price risks, based on standardised contracts and products. Further notified projects concern e.g. creating a Polish agri-food holding company or restoring and supporting the development of local agricultural markets. Work is currently underway at the Ministry of Development to verify the submitted projects. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In the next part of my speech, I will discuss the Council of the European Union's arrangements for agriculture and fisheries concerning the situation on the agricultural markets and the planned support measures. Ladies and gentlemen! Since 2015, I have been actively seeking additional funding from the EU budget within the European Union. to improve the situation on agricultural markets. As part of the debate in the EU Council on Agriculture and Fisheries, I have repeatedly stressed that specific decisions on the stabilisation of agricultural markets are needed today and should not be translated into a more explicit deadline. To this end, I have put forward a number of proposals in the EU for the milk, pigmeat and fruit and vegetables market. On 14 March 2016, at the request of Poland, a discussion was held on the market situation at the EU Council on Agriculture and Fisheries. During this meeting, I made demands concerning, among others. the re-introduction of temporary targeted direct support for milk and pigmeat producers, the increase of intervention prices for butter and milk powder, the mobilisation of export refunds for pigmeat and the increase of de minimis aid. The European Commission then announced extraordinary measures to support European farmers in the crisis, among others. by launching a fifth tranche of temporary support for fruit and vegetables producers, doubling the quantitative limits for intervention purchases of skimmed milk powder and butter, re-launching the aid for private storage of pigmeat in the time appropriate for the proper functioning of the market, allocating additional financial resources for promotion and information campaigns, strengthening the role of the producer in the supply chain, setting up a meat market observation centre. The proposals put forward by the Commission in principle were budgetary and legislative neutral, which means that they did not provide for additional support measures from the Union budget or a revision of the basic legislation. The proposals put forward by the Commission in principle... At the time, I argued that no additional appropriations were allocated from the EU budget. the crisis sectors, in particular the milk market, cannot be sufficiently supported. Time has shown that unfortunately Poland's predictions have come true. As a result, due to the persistent difficult situation on the milk market at the European Union Council for Agriculture and Fisheries on 18 July 2016, the European Commission presented an agricultural support package of EUR 500 million in July, which includes, first, a production reduction mechanism. It proposes to allocate EUR 150 million to support the voluntary reduction of milk supply in the European Union. The milk producer will decide to join the mechanism individually by declaring the level of reduction in milk supply which he will undertake to achieve in relation to the reference period. The mechanism will work at European Union level so that farmers in the European Union can access the programme under the same conditions. The support rate is proposed at EUR 14 per 100 kg of milk which will not be delivered for buying-in. Secondly, the so-called mechanism. conditional adjustment aid. It covers the amount of EUR 350 million allocated per national envelope. Poland is to get 6.5% this amount, that is EUR 22.7 million. The project envisages the possibility of supplementing the amount of aid granted by the national budget with a maximum of 100% the amount of Union funds. The aid will be paid to milk producers or, depending on the decision of the Member State, to farmers in other farming sectors upon compliance with certain requirements. Aid may be granted to producers who carry out one or more of the following activities: firstly, a reduction in milk production greater than that covered by financial aid granted at European Union level from the first programme, secondly, a small scale of production, thirdly, the use of extensive production methods, fourthly, the use of environmentally friendly production methods, fifthly, the implementation of joint projects, sixthly, the implementation of quality programmes aimed at promoting quality and added value, seventhly, training in financial instruments. These two mechanisms are the most important elements of the milk package. In addition, Member States were given the opportunity to amend the arrangements for voluntary coupled support for the dairy sector in order to ensure the separation of payments from production in 2017. Another element of the package is the extension of the public intervention period for skimmed milk powder until the end of February 2017, when the standard intervention period will resume. By the end of December 2016, the ceiling for the buying-out of skimmed milk powder shall be maintained at a fixed price of 350 000. tonnes. The application of the private storage aid scheme for skimmed milk powder will also be extended until the end of February 2017. It is also envisaged to extend the possibility of voluntary supply management by recognised producer organisations, their associations and interbranch organisations as well as dairy cooperatives. In addition, the Commission confirmed the update of the market withdrawal support by producer organisations in the fruit and vegetables sector, as well as an earlier payment of 70% of advances for direct payments and 85% for rural development area payments from 16 October 2016 following the completion of administrative controls. During the debate on the package, referring to the difficult market situation, I pointed out that the aid measures put forward by the Commission must be simple to implement and quick to apply so that the aid can be delivered to farmers as soon as possible. European Commission legislative acts will be issued in mid-September 2016, which will implement these proposals. Work on individual solutions has already begun at expert level. To sum up, the European Union's existing measures to improve the market situation have introduced, firstly, exceptional support measures in the milk and pigmeat sector amounting to EUR 28.9 million. In addition to the funding from the EU budget for the implementation of the support, we have allocated from the national budget an amount equal to EU funds, so that the total financial envelope available to support milk and pig producers amounted to PLN 245.7 million, after PLN 122.9 million for producers in the milk and pigmeat sectors, with support of PLN 91.5 thousand. milk producers and 63.1 thousand in the pigmeat sector. Secondly, aid for private storage of butter and skimmed milk powder, which has been implemented continuously since 2014. Thirdly, public intervention for butter and skimmed milk powder, of which Polish entrepreneurs directed more than 11% to intervention warehouses the total quantity of OMP. Fourthly, voluntary supply management - under the Commission regulations, provisions have been adopted allowing recognised producer organisations, their associations, recognised inter-branch organisations, as well as dairy cooperatives to make voluntary agreements and decide on the planning of milk production for the period from April to October 2016, when seasonal production increases occur in the sector. Fifthly, a temporary increase in State aid which would allow Member States to provide a maximum amount of 15 000. the euro per farmer per year without applying any national ceiling for holdings freezing or reducing production. Sixth, private storage of pigmeat from 4 January to 20 January 2016 After the seventh, IV extraordinary support mechanism, i.e. further temporary exceptional support for producers of certain fruit and vegetables due to the extension by the Russian Federation of the import ban on fruit and vegetables from the European Union countries - the total amount of support provided under the IV mechanism will be EUR 137.3 million. After the eighth, the V mechanism of extraordinary support in the fruit and vegetables sector, currently at the implementation stage in Polish legislation. After the ninth extension of the deadline for submitting payment applications in 2016, i.e. by 15 June 2016 After ten years, it has already been developed in the Ministry and adopted by the management a draft regulation on the possibility of borrowing interest-free 10-year loans to people who have problems with payments due. This regulation will be adopted by the government in the near future. The next steps will be based on the above-mentioned July agricultural package. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In the final part of the speech, I would like to discuss an important initiative taken in the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, which is the development programme of the main markets in Poland. The aim of the study is to develop a clear long-term approach for market participants to ensure that the identified objectives for the development of the national agri-food sector are met. The draft programme also aims to systematize and collect information on current initiatives and directions of actions enabling the development of markets in Poland. The programme focuses on nine agricultural markets, i.e. the markets for cereals, rape, pigmeat, beef, poultry, milk, milk products, sugar, fruit and vegetables and tobacco. In Chapter I, the draft programme provides an analysis of the various sectors, including data on their structure, level of production and foreign trade, thus drawing a picture of the conditions for the development of the individual markets. It also includes a sector-specific SWOT analysis. Chapter II discusses the main instruments available for EU and national support to agricultural markets. The instruments available in the framework of the common organisation of the markets in agricultural products, direct payments, the Rural Development Programme 2014-2020 and the elements of national aid were discussed. Chapter III sets out the objectives and challenges for each sector and proposes measures to be taken to achieve the objectives set. The current situation in the various agricultural sectors varies as regards the dynamics of changes in production levels, trends in foreign trade, the degree of organisation of producers and the overall condition of the sector. Whatever the case, there are challenges and opportunities for development in each sector. The action section is divided into general actions which will affect all agricultural sectors as well as actions specific to selected agricultural markets. For example, in the pork sector, reconstruction of pig population in Poland is one of the main challenges. This is a challenge that requires multi-directional action. The proposals for action in the programme indicate those that will facilitate the construction of new facilities, reduce administrative burdens, but also those that improve the consolidation of the sector. When analysing opportunities to support the development of agricultural markets and improve their functioning, it should be borne in mind that the current financial perspective under the common agricultural policy is already in place, the direct payments system and the Rural Development Programme 2014 Work on this programme at ministry level is currently being finalised. On completion of this work, the programme will be consulted on the agricultural environment. I am convinced that it will contribute to long-term work on improving the situation on agricultural markets. You received the draft program in print No. 760. The current situation on the agricultural markets, particularly in the milk and pigmeat markets, is dramatic. Low prices of agricultural fruits at high prices of fertilisers, feed, fuel and other means of production make agricultural production uneconomical. Would any of your fellow Members be able to work hard every day without a holiday and, at the end, not get paid, which may take months? Government and MEPs need to be stepped up to diversify farmers' incomes, for example. amendment of the RES Act or the adoption of a law on the use of habitats submitted by us for the purpose of conducting non-agricultural economic activities. The situation on both internal and external agricultural markets requires constant analysis of developments. It should be stressed that we do not have much influence on external markets, what is happening on the markets in Paris or Chicago is completely independent of our actions. Our activity should focus on the internal market and shape it in order to adapt in advance to and respond to changing conditions in the world. It is therefore necessary to take initiatives at national level to strengthen Poland's position as a European leader in the production of high-quality food. The situation on the pork market would significantly improve the food labelling law, which would allow the consumer to choose whether he wants to buy meat from Poland or from foreign raw materials. There are countries that have gone further in their regulations requiring retail networks to ensure that at least 51% of the food products offered for sale are available. national products. This law defines the domestic product as originating in the territory of a country whose basic raw materials originate in 100% of domestic holdings. The role of the so-called. a short supply chain, which assumes that 51% the food sold must come from regional and local suppliers. The Act also introduces strict rules on the labelling of goods for which serious penalty payments are punishable. In my opinion, there is a need in Poland to introduce similar regulations as this law from one of the countries of the European Union. Another example is France, which has been authorised by the European Commission to introduce compulsory labelling of the country of origin for dairy products and processed meat products. The aim of the legislation is to protect the French market. Lithuania, Italy, Portugal want to follow France and have already requested the European Commission to make similar arrangements. This is an example for us that we should not be afraid of the European Commission, but we should definitely present the Polish position and negotiate conditions hard. The current priorities of the Ministry of Agriculture should be such as to ensure equalisation of direct payments. During her exposé, Prime Minister Beata Szydło announced the subject of compensation, she stressed that it cannot be so that Polish farmers have different, worse working conditions than farmers in other countries of the European Union. In 2015, the Polish farmer could count on EUR 207 per hectare, while the German farmer received EUR 308, or almost 50%. more than received by the Polish farmer. As a result of the significant difference between the amount of subsidies, Polish farmers cannot compete equally in the European single market. I therefore call on the Prime Minister and the Minister to address this issue as soon as possible. Ladies and gentlemen! The Sejm's task as a body of the legislative authority is to create a law that will support agriculture, give opportunities for development and development opportunities, and not to multiply obstacles. At meetings, farmers often complain about such regulations - unclear, hampering the development of a private initiative, defending the privileges of large networks of supermarkets and corporations. The biggest benefit of hard work for farmers today is not from farmers, but from intermediaries who dictate the prices of agricultural products. The dictation of intermediaries means that farmers do not have an equal position in the negotiations, if they want to sell, they have to adapt to the price imposed. It is therefore necessary to create a law which will benefit agriculture, which will allow stable rural development, and farmers will create opportunities for decent income. This is the opportunity offered already in January by the Poselski Kukiz Club15 draft law on the sale of processed food by farmers. I often hear the question: How is it possible that in France, in Italy you can easily buy local cheeses, sausages, jams, wines, oil, and in Poland it is impossible, it is forbidden, farmers are being hunted? Unfortunately, the Polish State is overzealous in this respect and introduces more stringent regulations than those required by European Union law. Let's stop creating more overregulations. The second important project to improve the situation on the agricultural markets, which was recently submitted by my club, concerns the amendment of the Food Safety and Nutrition Act. The main objective of the proposed law is to introduce a voluntary mark free of GMOs. The consumer has the right to obtain reliable information about the composition of the product, as well as how it was manufactured. The introduction of GMO-free markings is intended to help implement this right. Austria, Germany, France, Slovenia, Luxembourg have similar positive labelling systems and work is already very advanced in Belgium and Hungary. The labelling of GMO-free products reflects a pan-European trend where more than 70% society is opposed to transgenic food. Therefore, my concern is the government draft law amending the Law on Feed, which entered the Sejm last week. The ban on GMO feed was introduced by law in 2006 and was due to enter into force in 2008. Unfortunately, the date of entry into force of the ban was further shifted by the PO-PSL coalition, to January 2013, later to January 2017, which was strongly criticised in previous terms by PiS Members. Meanwhile, the current project by the PiS government is pushing the deadline for the ban to enter into force for a further 4 years. According to the report of the CBOS of January 2013, the Commission concluded that it is necessary to assess the compatibility of the measures with the internal market. Food safety Poles and GMOs 65% is polled for the ban on GMO cultivation in Poland. I would like to ask the Minister: What is the reason for such a radical change of position? If there's going to be a change, it's going to be one that doesn't generate extra costs. I would also like to point out some of the problems identified by agricultural experts which appear or may arise in the near future. Firstly, a threat to the cereals and rape market where grain quality can be expected to fall. The impact of the Ukrainian market is an unfavorable factor for cereals and rape producers. Poland is the first country to which grain from Ukraine affects. Large facades used for metastatic purposes are created in our country. There are cases that ships that come to Poland are quickly loaded with Ukrainian grain, and then only Polish grain is added. This can cause a very difficult situation on the cereals market. I therefore call for protectionist measures to be taken, so that our services carefully check how much grain affects Poland, what is its quality and whether all transports are registered so that under the quota coat does not affect three times more cereals than the quota amount of Ukraine. Secondly, the quantity of fruit and vegetables fixed for Poland, which can be withdrawn from the market, is too low, which can further destabilise the fruit market, especially apples. Poland reduced the withdrawal limit from 286 thousand. t at 89,000. t, i.e. reduced by 70%, although the programme was implemented in accordance with procedures. Thirdly, the difficult situation of pig farmers is getting worse. There is a drastic decline in the population, especially sows, the stock base, to the lowest in the history of the state of 800,000. pcs This is the result of both low profitability of production and the growing epidemic of African swine fever - ASF. The government's actions, and especially the sanitary firing of wild boars, carriers of this disease, are insufficient. The population of boars grows dramatically, about six times over a few years, with about 60,000. Up to 400 thousand units. The firing plans do not take into account the fact that wild as living organisms are subject to reproduction law and under current climatic and environmental conditions - mild winters and living in a monoculture of maize - up to 16 are born from one female per year. At this rate of reproduction, the current firing plan will not reduce production, but will significantly increase production. Fourthly, even in the poultry market, where the situation is relatively best, there is a risk of a deep crisis due to the withdrawal from the GMO soybean perspective. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-22-sejm-23-4.u318	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! Thank you for inviting me to the Sejm. As every year, I will provide you with information on the relevant problems arising from the activities and case law of the Constitutional Tribunal. This is the year 2015. The information was adopted by the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal in April this year. and passed on to the Sejm. I trust you would like to familiarize yourselves with this detailed information contained in Seym printing No. 470. Last year was a record for the Constitutional Court in terms of the number of cases decided and decisions given. The Court settled a total of 303 cases by 93% more than in 2014. Some cases were examined jointly by the Court and issued a total of 173 substantive judgments, i.e. 63 judgments and 110 provisions to terminate the proceedings. The number of substantive judgments was higher by 45% than that of 2014. Among 63 judgments in 35 of them, the Court ruled that one or more of the contested provisions were unconstitutional. In 10 cases, the Court postponed the expiry date of the non-constitutional provisions in force. The length of the substantive examination of the case was 3 months longer than in 2014 and was 21 months. In addition, the Court issued 5 signalling provisions to ensure the consistency of the legal system by the law-making bodies. At the preliminary stage of the review of constitutional complaints and requests by limited legitimacy entities, the Court issued a total of 918 provisions and regulations, i.e. 11% More than a year ago. The Court provided 10% for its substantive examination constitutional complaints and 50% requests from entities with limited legitimacy. I also wanted to draw your attention to some of the most important judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal last year. I will mention only the most important of them, representative of the two basic functions of the tribunal - as guardian of freedom and human and citizen rights and as guardian of the constitutional foundations of the Republic of Poland. In the first of these categories, the constitution plays the role of a shield for man and citizen, in the second - the ponytail of the Polish state system. Among the most important judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal in matters of freedom and human rights and the citizen last year are undoubtedly those in matters of open pension funds and the constitutional right of citizens to social security, a clause of conscience for doctors, a free amount reducing income tax on individuals and the protection of the minimum existence of citizens, a banking enforcement title, the absence of a time limit for annulment of a decision taken in breach of the law, freedom of association in trade unions and financial supervision of cooperative savings and credit banks. In turn, the most important judgments of last year's Court relating to the basis of the regime of the Republic of Poland should be regarded as those relating to the principle of division, balance and cooperation of the authorities and to the principle of independence of courts, independence of judges, including judgments on the amendment of the Law on the System of General Courts, on the immunity of judges in the mirror procedure and on two judgments in the Constitutional Tribunal Act - of 3 December 2015 on certain provisions of the 2015 Court Act in its original wording and of 9 December 2015 on the November amendment of the Law on the Court of First Instance. The Law of 22 December 2015 amended the Law of 22 December 2015 which governs the organisation of the Constitutional Tribunal and the procedure for proceedings before it. The constitutionality of that law was ruled by the Court in its judgment of 9 March 2016 - from 134 days not published despite the constitutional requirement. This state of affairs, the fact of not publishing has not obtained any approval of Polish judges, practitioners of law. With regard to these three judgments on the Court of First Instance and its successive amendments, I am deeply sorry to say that the formation of the statutory basis for the functioning of the Constitutional Tribunal, which contains content which does not comply with the Constitution, and that it is very difficult for the Court to establish them in a constitutional procedure. This is due to the problem of staffing, i.e. the President's failure to take the oath from the three judges of the Tribunal, although in his judgment of 3 December the Tribunal boundly decided on the constitutionality of the contested element of the procedure for the selection of this group of Judges and the election by the Sejm of the current term of office of three Judges to the seats previously effectively filled by the Sejm of the previous term of office. This is an exceptional situation in the history of the Constitutional Tribunal, very difficult for the tribunal. We try to work normally, although the absence of three judges ruling and the refusal to pronounce judgments of the tribunal in official journals make this work very difficult for us and also for Polish courts. I ask you to remember that the efficient Constitutional Tribunal is an integral part of the Polish state's system, which is defined in the Constitution. If this system wants to change, it can be done by changing the constitution in the procedure provided for by it, not by paralyzing the body that is supposed to guard compliance with this constitution. These are standards of civilized states, and Poland should belong to such countries. Ladies and gentlemen, two months ago, it was 30 years since the Constitutional Tribunal issued its first ruling in which it ruled that the contested regulation of the Council of Ministers was incompatible with the Constitution and thus proved, at the outset, that it would not be a facade institution. As a result, the Constitutional Tribunal played a role in the bloodless dismantling of the communist system at the end of the 1980s. It is with great sadness that I must say that the recent legislative decisions of the Sejm seem to be an attempt to achieve an unfulfilled dream from the time of the formation of the tribunal, i.e. the wish of the Constitutional Court as a tool exclusively legitimising the political party's power. The work on the next Court Act started on 29 April 2016 in a manner that did not correspond to the seriousness of the Polish state. In its law, the Sejm introduced non-constitutional solutions, contrary to the case law of the Court and ignoring the basic recommendations of the Venice Commission of 11 March 2015. This is an expression of disregard for the standards of the western culture of law in the name of the implementation of the current political will. The Sejm shall be in accordance with Article. 197 constitutions have the power to adopt a very important law defining the organisation of the tribunal and the procedure for proceedings before the tribunal. However, this does not mean that such a law may have any substance, its standards may not paralyze the Court in the exercise of its basic functions as defined in Article. 188, Art. 189 and Article 18 (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [2] shall apply mutatis mutandis. 193 constitutions, i.e. ruling on the compatibility of the law with the constitution. Such a law must be subject to immediate review by the Court and its standards must not prevent judgment in this case. Legal standards cannot deprive judges of the right to be subject to only a constitution in the exercise of their office, as expressly provided for in Article. 195 constitutions. I will repeat once again: power, including legislative power, must not be everything. Power is bound by the constitution - every power, every subsequent Sejm is bound by the fundamental foundations of our constitutional democracy enshrined in the law. No Sejm can change the basis of the regime of the Republic of Poland, no one can harm fundamental rights. The caselaw of the Constitutional Tribunal as a court of law upholds these rights. The nation also exercises power through the case law of the Constitutional Court. As constitutional judges, we have a deep sense of that. The Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, drawn up by 10 countries and drawn up in Rome on 4 November 1950, states in the last recital setting out the common deep-rooted European values: (z) decided as Governments of European States, acting in the same spirit and having a common heritage of ideals and political traditions of respect for freedom and rule of law. In constitutional democracy there is the rule of law, not the will of the individual. Ladies and gentlemen! It so happens that the deadline for submitting annual information to the President of the Constitutional Tribunal was the sad day for the democratic rule of law to adopt a new law on the Constitutional Court containing significant constitutional defects, including those which the Tribunal has repeatedly ruled on in previous months. This next act is another act of instrumentalization of the law governing the system of an important state institution. In order to achieve the political goal, the law is broken: our tribunal, which will not prevent us from implementing our programme. There is no need to fear the tribunal, there is no need to fear the wrong law and the destruction of the rule of law. Mr President's close co-workers have been announcing for several weeks that if a satisfactory solution is not reached in Parliament, the President of the Republic will in due course come into the matter of the Constitutional Tribunal. Mr. President! This moment has just arrived. I allow myself to appeal to you to veto the Constitutional Tribunal Act adopted today or to refer it before signing to the Constitutional Tribunal in order to examine the compatibility with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. This will be a lasting, even academic example of the cooperation of executive, legislative and judicial authorities in our country. By virtue of Article 2 (1) of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L So you're a guard, among other things. respect for the principle of separation and balance of authorities, as well as the protection of constitutional freedoms and human rights and of the citizen. Please take advantage of these competences. Taking this opportunity, and this is my last speech in front of the High House, by providing information on the important problems arising from the case law of the Constitutional Tribunal, I thank each Member and every Member who today, but not only today, have expressed their attachment to constitutionalism: democracy, freedom and the rule of law. Thank you to every lawyer working in the grandstand. They form a corps of excellent specialists in all areas of law to assist constitutional judges. I would like to thank all the staff of the Court's office. Thanks also to them, our constitutional court enjoys authority in Poland and abroad. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

This was the first time during the reporting period, i.e. In 2015, the Authority of state bodies requires, in particular, strict compliance with the principles of the threefold division and balance of the authorities. The President of the Constitutional Tribunal's information on the significant problems arising from the Court's case-law in 2015 refers in detail to the adoption on 25 June 2015 of a new law on the Constitutional Tribunal repealing the former, after 18 years of its validity, as well as the legal consequences of its entry into force and the election of five new judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. However, this information ignores the direct participation of certain judges of the Tribunal in the legislative work on the June 2015 Act, which subsequently resulted in the exclusion from the ruling on the constitutionality of that law. It is widely accepted to maintain rules guaranteeing impartiality of judicial proceedings that no one should be a judge in his own case - the old Roman rule says: nemo iudex in causa sua. The 137th June Act... [[I think it's 2015.]] ...for the election of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal in place of five judges, which ended in November and December 2015 with a 9-year term of office of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, but did not take any position on this matter, did not express a legal opinion - they simply deliberately ignored the matter. So far, they have watched over every article in this bill. The resolution of the Sejm of 8 October 2015 on the election of five judges of the Constitutional Tribunal was deprived of legal force by the resolution of the Sejm of 25 November. The president took a vow from five judges. They were elected legally and all, not only two of them, should carry out the duties of judge of the Constitutional Tribunal. However, three judges are not allowed to adjudicate in the Court for more than 8 months, even though they are still receiving compensation. It is not apparent from the Constitutional Court Act that judges should be remunerated for their readiness to rule. The persons elected as Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal on 8 October are not judges because their choice of the Sejm has been declared devoid of legal force and the President of the Republic has not taken the oath from them. Therefore, it is impossible to speak of October judges, because these are not judges of the Constitutional Tribunal... [[Bullshit.]] ...only those elected to judge. [[It's a lie.]] Many representatives of constitutional law recognise the procedure for the election of a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal in such a way that it ends with the acceptance of a vow or the taking of a vow by the President of the Republic. The appeal to the President of the Republic, as the opposition does, to take the oath away from the persons who were elected as Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal on 8 October, which has lost its legal force, is in fact a call for the President of the Republic to commit a constitutional delict... [[He's in a constant detour.]] ...in particular, since the Sejm, in its resolution of 8 October, asked the President to refrain from accepting the oath from the persons referred to in the five resolutions of 8 October 2015, to elect judges. The resolutions of the Sejm of 8 October were subject to constitutional review and the Constitutional Tribunal maintained the existing line of case law that the Sejm's resolutions do not fall within the scope of the Constitutional Tribunal's cognition and by the order of 7 January 2016 dismissed the proceedings. It was about U8/15. After this judgment, the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal Julia Przyłębska and Piotr Pszczółkowski were allowed to rule. It is also worth recalling the case of a precedent issue on 30 November 2015 of the freezing order, at the request of a group of opposition Members, by the Constitutional Tribunal calling on the Sejm to suspend the procedure for the act amending the Court's Law of June 2015. In our view, the Club of Law and Justice, there is no basis for the proper application of the provisions of the Code of Civil Procedure in this respect, the law on the safeguarding of the action, because no claim by the non-essential Constitutional Tribunal had. Under what rules would he have a claim to the Sejm in this regard? Two amendments to the Constitutional Tribunal Act of November and December 2015 were the subject of constitutional review. In its first judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal held on 3 December 2015 that the basis for the election of three Judges replacing those Judges whose term of office expired before the end of the previous term of office of the Sejm was valid and the President of the Republic was obliged to take the oath from those Judges. The basis for the selection of the other two judges was, in turn, considered to be unconstitutional. Attention should be paid to the legal basis of the choice rather than to the choice itself. In public space, there is a definition that three judges have been validly elected and the choice of two has not been valid. The Constitutional Tribunal did not take this into account in its statement of reasons the 8th term of office of the Sejm on 2 December of the election of five Judges as Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal and of their taking the oath to the President of the Republic. However, in the justification for that judgment, the Court noted that, under certain exceptional circumstances, there could be situations which, objectively, would order the President to protect a higher value than the immediate fulfilment of the obligation to take a vow, which constitutes the primacy of the Constitution. Was this not the case after 8 October 2015? The President does not go beyond the powers conferred on him and does not violate the principle of legalism, but leaves the competent authorities to resolve the doubts raised. Parliament, when adopting the June Act on the Constitutional Court, introduced rules which were different from those of the Sejm. The opposition simply did not have time to adapt the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm to the wording of the article. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 June 1st Act. That's, among other things. was the basis for recognition by the resolutions of 25 November that those resolutions of 8 October 2015 on selection have no legal force. Like we'll never run a computer program that contains conflicting instructions, no matter how long they've pressed enter, we can't do internal conflicting law. This is the only way out of this situation. They were eliminated from legal circulation as a non-constitutional judgment of the Court of 9 March 2016, which the Government does not want to publish in the Official Journal to date. The Sejm, when adopting this legal basis, did not maintain the constitutional obligation to consider the draft law in three readings and to proceed in violation of a number of rules of the Sejm. The justification for the judgment of the Court stresses that Parliament has infringed the principle of legalism and the principle of correct legislation. There have also been violations of constitutional standards concerning the legislative initiative, amendments to the draft law, while the absence of a vacathio legis law in December was intended to prevent the tribunal from preparing from the organisational side to the changes introduced by the amendment and at the same time to prevent its constitutionality from being controlled. The Venice Commission considered that the ongoing reduction in the efficiency of the Constitutional Tribunal in Poland undermines the three fundamental principles of the Council of Europe: democracy, human rights and the rule of law, and thus is unacceptable, as also underlined by the Constitutional Tribunal in its report. One of the foundations of the state system is the principle of division, balance and cooperation between the three authorities: legislative, executive and judicial. The Constitutional Court has a particular role to play among the judiciary. It's a role in controlling the constitutionality of the legislator's proceedings. However, the effective control can only be carried out by a fully independent, independent and independent authority. The Court is to protect us, citizens, from the law which is adopted by Parliament and which undermines our constitutional rights and freedoms, but also from the principles of correct legislation. The Court is from protecting us against bad law - evil both because it threatens or harms our interests, and because it is wrongly and sloppyly passed, and thus it is difficult for the authorities and courts to apply in practice, i.e. one that is simply a legal bubble, and such laws from the Polish parliament are coming out more and more. Yes, ladies and gentlemen, it is the right to decide what the representatives of the authority are allowed to do and what they are not allowed to do. I am not saying that this analogy fully reflects reality, but it shows the direction, shows who, for what and for what it can serve and what the paralysis of the Constitutional Tribunal can lead to. It is regrettable that not the results of the work, but the legal chaos around the Constitutional Court has put a shadow on the activities of the Constitutional Tribunal last year. Many institutions, organizations, both in the country and in the world, express great concern about the state of constitutional order in Poland in connection with the events that have taken place in recent weeks and months. According to these opinions, it is emphasized that the Polish constitution was adopted by the people through a referendum and thus constitutes a good of special value, it is the highest law of the Republic of Poland. Unless otherwise specified, it may be applied directly. Its duty to respect it is primarily the responsibility of public authorities and those with positions in them. This means not only banning its open breach, but also circumvention of its rules and standards by using unreliable interpretation methods and using existing gaps. The Constitution must not be interpreted by anybody in such a way as to achieve only an ad hoc political advantage and to disregard the particularities of its interpretation. The deepening constitutional crisis is the consequence of omission in the interpretation of constitutional norms, axiological arguments or theoretical assumptions underlying its provisions. It does not serve Poland, which ceases to be a common good, nor its citizens, it also leads to a reduction in the legal, political, but also generally understood culture. We would not want to go back to the chronology of the crisis, which is obvious today, but to focus on the effects of its continuation, because it seems that these effects are of greatest concern. The first and very important thing is the loss of Poland's prestige due to the fact that the tribunal, the constitutional court has been compromised. This prestige has been built for many years, but unfortunately in a short period of time he has lost a lot. These effects are also a constant concern about legal duality related to non-respect, defective publication of judgments of the Court. This concern, which has been expressed in many local governments as representatives of public administration, is best demonstrated. And perhaps the most important effect that we should have in mind somewhere is fundamental - faultyly functioning Constitutional Court is the lack of protection of fundamental rights and civil liberties of Poles and Poles. That's what's most concerned. It is not possible today to refer to what has happened today in this House, namely once again to adopt a new regulation concerning the Constitutional Tribunal, supposedly a comprehensive regulation - there are different opinions here - but there is no difference in what has left the tribunal in such a state of inertia, a crisis. This is the complete omission of matters relating to judges in a faulty manner appointed for their duties, that is also the complete omission of the issue of unpublished judgments of the tribunal. So we have, in short, a new regulation, which, apart from the fact that there are a lot of constitutional doubts, today the previous speakers have pointed out this, goes completely away from these two key problems that have grown in recent weeks and which are effectively paralyzing the functioning of the tribunal in some way. I would also like to use this day to express my appreciation on behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club for this last year of the Constitutional Tribunal's activities, a very difficult year, and also to emphasize what has already happened today - the steadfastness you have shown. You have taken great responsibility for the institution you represent, and it is highly respectful. But given that 2016 is the last year of operation, Mr President, as your manager, I also wanted to thank you for all your work - on your hands, but also on your coworkers. This contribution... You can't overestimate the last few years, and these indicators, which you're presenting, and I've allowed myself to replicate them, prove it best. The Court serves Poles, Poles and Poles very well, and this is a key, fundamental thing that needs to be highlighted today. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-22-sejm-23-4.u347	Thank you very much. Mr Jacek Wilk, Kukiz.15.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-22-sejm-23-4.u348	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The National Bank of Poland issued an order forcing individual buyers of investment gold to provide personal data. Individual buyers of gold coins shall be required to provide data enabling them to be identified, in particular their name, surname and PESEL number. Similar restrictions, resulting from the VAT Act, already apply to private sellers. The question arises: What is the need for authority, who, when, for how much and from whom did he buy a gold bar or coin, and how much? History teaches that such knowledge in the hands of the state is always dangerous and can be used against the owners of the crumb. In the apogee of the Great Depression, on April 5, 1933, President Franklin Delano Roosevelt released the so-called. Implementing Regulation No 6102. Under it, every U.S. citizen was ordered to exchange all his gold for paper dollars, allowing only less than five ounces of crumbling. Next thing you know, a dollar devaluation was made. Of course, the people of the United States have widely ignored the order to sell gold, and the owners of more gold have transported it abroad. If that authority knew who had the gold and how much it had, it would certainly use that knowledge to effectively rob citizens. The bans and restrictions were also applied by Stalinist governments. On 28 October 1950 in Poland gold was banned in the form of coins and bars. Fifteen years in prison for breaking that ban. What is the purpose of the Polish authorities today to gather knowledge about gold holders? In anticipation of the new financial crisis, are they looking for the opportunity to rob the owners of crumbs? Unfortunately, this cannot be ruled out, and even if the current authority does not yet have such plans, it will not guarantee that in the future another government will not use the information it has obtained to mass-confiscate gold or even its forced redemption for worthless paper money. The reason for gathering this information may also be the desire to gain additional leverage on more cost-effective citizens. When the government speaks more and more about the need for individual retirement savings, it seems that the authorities want to have full control over citizens' savings at the same time. Or maybe a team of good changes simply realizes in this way their manic need to surveillance everyone and everything, expressed in the so-called records. An anti-terrorist bill? I believe that all ladies and gentlemen are familiar with George Orwell's novel of 1984. It is worth recalling, then, that this novel was written for caution rather than as a textbook. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-22-sejm-23-4.u352	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! July 22nd this year It's over 135. anniversary of the birth of Bolesław Wieniawy-Długoszowski, Maj-Gen of the Polish Army, diplomat, freemason, personal adjutant Józef Piłsudski. Bolesław Wieniawa-Długoszowski grew up in the town of Bobowa near Gorlice. In 1900 he passed his M.A. exam at the Higher Secondary School. Jan Długosz in Nowy Sącz. Although he had artistic talents, he decided to study at the Faculty of Medicine of the University of Lviv, which he graduated with honours. In 1906, Wieniawa-Długoszowski married Stefania Calvas, an opera singer with whom he left for Berlin. After a year he moved to Paris and became one of the founders of the Polish Artists' Society. He also started working in the Sagittarius branch, and that's when he met Józef Piłsudski. After the outbreak of World War I, Wieniawa-Długoszowski came to Kraków. Two days after the start of the course in the Shooting Union, he received a call to 1. The Personnel Company. Then it belonged to 1. Regiment of the Polish Legions of Ulans. He was promoted to lieutenant for fighting. After a special mission in Warsaw, he became a personal aide to Józef Piłsudski. He was released from the army in 1917. Later, as a member of the Polish Military Organization, he was arrested and imprisoned. After regaining his freedom, he participated in the preparation of the Vilnius expedition and the Kiev campaign. He also participated in the Battle of Warsaw. During the war with the Bolsheviks, he commanded cavalry units. He was then promoted to the rank of lieutenant colonel. In November 1921 Wieniawa-Długoszowski took part in the work on the Polish-Romanian convention. After a year's break, he returned to duty, first in 19. Volynski Ulanów Regiment and then in 1. Szwolelerz regiment Józef Piłsudski in Warsaw. In 1925 he completed a training course and received a scientific diploma from an officer of the General Staff. He then became a referee at Army Inspector No. II in Warsaw. After a year, he was moved to 1. The Szwolerozer regiment was appointed commander of the regiment and later appointed First Chief Officer of Staff at the General Inspector of the Armed Forces. He served as Commander for seven years. Cavalry Division and 1. Cavalry Brigades in Warsaw. In 1937, he was appointed commander of the Cavalry Division in Warsaw. He was promoted to a division general with first place in the corps of generals and served until 1941. That's when he was transferred to diplomacy. In 1938 Wieniawa-Długoszowski became an ambassador of the Republic of Poland in Rome. The President of the Republic of Poland Ignacy Mościcki appointed him his successor in case the office was emptied before the conclusion of the room. In opposition to the opposition of France, Great Britain and the anti-Pilgrims, he resigned his office. In 1942, Wieniawa-Długoszowski was nominated as a Member of the Republic of Poland in Cuba. It was a blow to the honor of the general, who could not accept the withdrawal from the Polish war. Probably because of this, the General took his own life on July 1st. The circumstances of his death have not yet been clearly defined. Wieniawa-Długoszowski's funeral took place in New York. His ashes were brought to Rakowicki Cemetery in Krakow in 1990. General Wieniawa-Długoszowski received many awards, among others. The Silver Cross of the Order of Virtuti Militari, the Commander's Cross with the Star of the Order of Rebirth of Poland, the Commander's Cross of the Order of Rebirth of Poland and the Fighting Cross - four times. Thank you.

It was too late to organize any help. The troubled priest, who had been in such a situation for the first time in his life, must have been a midwife and a nurse.
