ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-22-sejm-23-4.u356	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Grunwald, a small village in the district of Ostródz, where a great battle took place on 15 July 1410. Each Pole knows the date and meaning of this battle. This battle had a huge impact on our country's history. In this battle, the combined Polish-Lithuanian-Russian forces, approx. 29 thousand. The army of the Teutonic Order, totaled 21,000. Armed. The Teutonic Order, whose task was to convert the Gentiles with fire and sword, and often with trickery, intrigues, took piece by piece the lands of Christian Poland. The Teutonic Order also seized part of the Lithuanian Żmuda. The conduct of the Order was marked by the territorial expansion carried out by all possible means. Over the years, the Order used the weakness of the Polish state, which was experiencing the break-up of the district, and only during the reign of Władysław Jagiełła, when Poland was once again united, and its ally was the Grand Duchy of Lithuania, with all its power began to confront the military with the Order. The war was inevitable. Such conflicts are often referred to as a great war. The Grunwald battle was one of the biggest battles of medieval Europe in terms of the number of participants. Both parties realized that war was only a matter of time, so they made haste preparations for it. The Teutonic Order developed hostile propaganda on a large scale in relation to Poland and its king. Among other things, they were accused. The king's presumably baptized and is still a pagan, Poland was called a barbaric, wild country. The Order intended to recruit as many knights as possible from Western Europe, translating the armed fight against Poland as the sacred duty of every Christian knight. It was to be a new crusade against the Gentiles. Thanks to these procedures, the Order was able to gather a very large army for those times. The religious army was formed by knights from almost all parts of Western Europe, but the core was German knights. As you can see, propaganda already in the 15th century played a huge role in politics and was no less important than armed forces or defense fortifications. A huge success of the Polish king Władysław Jagiełła before the coming war was the military alliance with the Grand Duchy of Lithuania. Władysław Jagiełło proved to be a great statesman, a great king, and also an excellent commander. Although he has been in conflict with his uncle's great Lithuanian prince Witold many times, he has led to an alliance, and Lithuanian-Russian troops have greatly contributed to the victory at Grunwald. And by the way: in later years Prince Witold continued to have various conflicts with the king of Poland, i.e., as history teaches, in the family is different. Thanks to the king's procedures, they were able to gather a very large army. The army was multinational and multicultural. The majority of forces were Polish knights and Lithuanian troops, but there were also Russian regiments, Moldovan troops, Czech mercenaries, Silesia and Tartars, refugees from the Golden Orda. Despite the religious fanaticism prevailing in the Middle Ages, the king of Poland gathered and led Catholics, Orthodox and even Muslims into battle. This shows the king's great leadership and diplomatic abilities. It can be said that Władysław Jagiełło was one of the best rulers of Poland, although, ironically, he was not a Pole. Wise governments, the proper decisions of the descendants of King Władysław made Poland the greatest European power in the 16th century. As a curiosity, I would like to mention the fact that the bridge was largely involved in the Battle of Grunwald. Poles were able to construct a skating bridge, which was more than 500 m long. This bridge was divided into individual elements and then transported and folded under Czerwiński, where the Polish army crossed the Vistula and joined the Lithuanian-Russian troops. The rapid crossing of Polish troops was a huge surprise for the Teutonic Knights - it thwarted their war plans. The construction of a large, solid and durable bridge proves the high technical level of the craftsmen of that time. I will not represent the course of battle, I would just like to point out that the Battle of Grunwald was a great military defeat of the Teutonic Order. This battle also demonstrated the great talent of the commander of King Władysław Jagiełła. Thanks to his wise and discreet decisions, victory was complete. Only a few opponents escaped from the battlefield. The Teutonic Order never again regained its former power. The defeat at Grunwald also caused that knights from western Europe were already so eager to assist the Order in fighting in the coming years. The Order could only count on mercenaries. After the battle, King Władysław gave an order to send the bodies of the great master Ulrich von Jungingen and other much more fallen religious brothers to the Teutonic Knights to Malbork. This proves the king's great generosity and thus contradicts what the Teutonic Orders preached, that he was a barbarian and a savage. The battle was won by King Władysław's army, but the war continued. Unfortunately, King Władysław's army failed to secure Malbork despite several months of siege. It can be said that despite the glorious victory at Grunwald, it was not fully exploited. The peace with the Teutonic Knights brought only small territorial benefits. There were many reasons for the fact that the Order was not completely defeated. In any event, under favourable circumstances, the religious state could be completely vanquished, but this unfortunately did not happen. This took revenge on the next generations of Poles who had to wage war against the religious state. The 13-year-old war waged in 1454 and 1466 by Kazimierz Jagiellonian, son of the great King Władysław, led to a defeat of the religious state, but also not entirely. Part of the land of the Order has been incorporated into Poland, the remaining lands have become the culprit of the Kingdom of Poland. Despite the possibility to incarnate all the lands of the Law into the Crown, this was not done. As time passed, the religious state became the Prussian state. Short-sighted and erroneous decisions of the next Polish rulers caused Prussia to cease to be a languish of Poland and become an independent state. The right policy of the rulers of Prussia, e.g. Frederick the Great has led this country to military power. The country, which has been militaryly defeated by the Polish Crown so many times and became its culprit in 1772, was one of the three countries that cut Poland. To sum up, military success does not always go hand in hand with far-sighted politics. Grunwald was a great victory that was practically not used. I believe that a great statesman, a politician, can serve his country just as well or even better than many prominent leaders. I would like to add, by the way, that throughout the 15th century the day of the victory was celebrated in the Polish Church on 15 July. It was the first national holiday in the Polish Kingdom and the first Polish festival celebrated as a political anniversary in the Church. Thank you.
